# PROVENANCE — Wave 97 Ship 1F cite-trail header (closes Wave 52 iter1c "UNCLEAR-PROVENANCE")
#
# Class: B — Novel eee-side glue with TIER-1 substrate (per Wave 97 fan-3 X3 archaeology
# at tmp/wave97-fan3-X3-hooks-rewrite-archaeology-2026-05-08.md — KEEP-WITH-CITE-IMPORT-AMBER
# per CLAUDE.md Section 14.5).
#
# WHY THIS SCRIPT EXISTS (cardinal-rule-7 "REPORT errors before routing around them"):
# Windows-specific runtime workaround for openai-codex plugin path-mangling bug. CC injects
# `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` in POSIX form `/z/claude-sota-installed/...` on Windows; Node's
# require() resolution then fails on hook scripts because the path isn't a valid Win32
# path. Upstream codex-plugin-cc has the .mjs source files (TIER-1 substrate) but ships
# NO rewriter for this Windows-specific interaction. This script is the eee-side runtime
# durability layer.
#
# TIER-1 SOTA SUBSTRATE CITES (TIER-1-DIRECT per CR-1; full HEAD SHA + line range + symbol-anchor):
# - Reference: Z:/repos/deps/codex-plugin-cc/plugins/codex/scripts/stop-review-gate-hook.mjs:142-157#main @ HEAD 807e03ac9d5aa23bc395fdec8c3767500a86b3cf
# - Reference: Z:/repos/deps/codex-plugin-cc/plugins/codex/scripts/session-lifecycle-hook.mjs:76-102#handleSessionStart+handleSessionEnd @ HEAD 807e03ac9d5aa23bc395fdec8c3767500a86b3cf
# - Reference: Z:/repos/deps/codex-plugin-cc/plugins/codex/scripts/lib/broker-lifecycle.mjs:43-56#sendBrokerShutdown @ HEAD 807e03ac9d5aa23bc395fdec8c3767500a86b3cf
#
# CITE-IMPORT-AMBER provenance (TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION per citation-discipline.md rule #8):
# - constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ codex-plugin-cc substrate above (function-symbol-anchored, full SHA), TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED @ sibling claude-sota commit 46358c2 (Wave 50 Fire 46 origin) + d298d47 (Wave 50 Fire 43 path-mangling actual-fix) + Wave 80 P#3 extension]
# - effective_tier = TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION (MIN_PRECEDENCE due to eee-local glue: Win32 path absolutization + version-globbed cache discovery + idempotent backup + dual-runtime targeting; HONEST-NON-FINDING gate per CR-12 step (iii) satisfied — mcp__github__search_code "openai/codex-plugin-cc plugin-hook-bootstrap rewrite" total_count=0)
#
# Wave 80 patch sentinels in this file (symbol-anchor; line numbers volatile per port-note-discipline §1):
# - WAVE80_191_SENTINEL marker — patches stop-review-gate-hook.mjs#main fast-exit ordering
# - WAVE80_245_SENTINEL marker — patches session-lifecycle-hook.mjs init flow
# - patch_191_stop_review_gate_hook() function applies #191 patch
# - find_mjs_files() function discovers targets across runtime cache + marketplaces
#
# RETIREMENT CONDITION:
# This script retires when openai/codex-plugin-cc upstream lands a Windows-aware POSIX→Win32
# path resolver in plugin-hook-bootstrap (and ships fixes for issues #191 + #245). File
# upstream issue at https://github.com/openai/codex referencing the path-mangling failure

"""Wave 50 Fire 46 — durability layer for openai-codex plugin path-mangling fix.

Per bog92qxq7 codex T1 BRIDGE-MODE real GPT-5.5 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.91 verdict
[VERIFIED via .claude/state/codex_consult_fire45_path_mangling_full_rescue_OUT.txt
EOF (5888 lines, Trust Boundary: top-level Codex investigation, NOT Sonnet stand-in)].
Pattern A prescribed_edits per codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md.

Idempotent + version-globbed + cache+marketplace + both-runtime + --check mode.
Re-applies Fire 43+44 absolute Win32 path patches on every launcher pre-claude.exe
invocation, ensuring durability across plugin re-fetches and version bumps.

Sister durability layer to Fire 45 (settings.json relocations a6c5e34 + 18fdbf0).

USAGE:
  python codex-plugin-hooks-rewrite.py            # rewrite all (default)
  python codex-plugin-hooks-rewrite.py --check    # verify only; exit 1 if drift
  python codex-plugin-hooks-rewrite.py --quiet    # suppress per-file output

Wired to: sss.ps1 + eee.ps1 launcher pre-claude.exe call (fail-closed exit 2).
"""
import argparse
import json
import pathlib
import re
import sys

NODE_EXE_PATH = "Z:\\tools\\nodejs\\node.exe"


def find_hooks_json_files():
    """Discover all openai-codex hooks.json files across both runtimes (version-globbed)."""
    results = []
    for runtime in ["Z:/claude-sota", "Z:/claude-sota-installed"]:
        # Cache: version-globbed under codex/<version>/
        cache_root = pathlib.Path(f"{runtime}/.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex")
        if cache_root.is_dir():
            for ver_dir in sorted(cache_root.iterdir()):
                if ver_dir.is_dir():
                    hj = ver_dir / "hooks" / "hooks.json"
                    if hj.exists():
                        results.append(hj)
        # Marketplace: single canonical path
        mp = pathlib.Path(
            f"{runtime}/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/openai-codex/plugins/codex/hooks/hooks.json"
        )
        if mp.exists():
            results.append(mp)
    return results


def derive_plugin_root_win32(hooks_json_path):
    """plugin_root = hooks_json.parent.parent, in Win32 backslash form."""
    return str(hooks_json_path.parent.parent).replace("/", "\\")


def build_hooks_dict(plugin_root_win32):
    sessionhook = plugin_root_win32 + "\\scripts\\session-lifecycle-hook.mjs"
    stophook = plugin_root_win32 + "\\scripts\\stop-review-gate-hook.mjs"
    return {
        "description": (
            "PATCHED Wave 50 Fire 46 — absolute Win32 paths bypass CC's POSIX-form "
            "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT} injection on Windows. Per bog92qxq7 codex T1 "
            "BRIDGE-MODE real GPT-5.5 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.91. Re-applied by "
            "codex-plugin-hooks-rewrite.py via sss.ps1 + eee.ps1 launchers "
            "pre-claude.exe (fail-closed). Sister: settings.json registrations "
            "a6c5e34 + 18fdbf0. Backup: hooks.json.pre-fire46-fix."
        ),
        "hooks": {
            "SessionStart": [{
                "hooks": [{
                    "type": "command",
                    "command": f'"{NODE_EXE_PATH}" "{sessionhook}" SessionStart',
                    "timeout": 5,
                }]
            }],
            "SessionEnd": [{
                "hooks": [{
                    "type": "command",
                    "command": f'"{NODE_EXE_PATH}" "{sessionhook}" SessionEnd',
                    "timeout": 5,
                }]
            }],
            "Stop": [{
                "hooks": [{
                    "type": "command",
                    "command": f'"{NODE_EXE_PATH}" "{stophook}"',
                    "timeout": 900,
                }]
            }],
        },
    }


def rewrite(verbose=True):
    files = find_hooks_json_files()
    if not files:
        print("WARN: no openai-codex hooks.json files found", file=sys.stderr)
        return 0
    rewrites = 0
    skips = 0
    for hj in files:
        plugin_root = derive_plugin_root_win32(hj)
        new_dict = build_hooks_dict(plugin_root)
        try:
            current = json.loads(hj.read_text())
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            current = None
        if current == new_dict:
            if verbose:
                print(f"SKIP (already correct): {hj}")
            skips += 1
            continue
        backup = hj.with_suffix(hj.suffix + ".pre-fire46-fix")
        if not backup.exists():
            backup.write_bytes(hj.read_bytes())
            if verbose:
                print(f"BACKUP: {backup}")
        hj.write_text(json.dumps(new_dict, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
        if verbose:
            print(f"REWROTE: {hj}")
        rewrites += 1
    if verbose:
        print(f"DONE: {rewrites} rewritten, {skips} already correct")
    return 0


def check():
    """Exit 1 if any hook command has ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}, /z/, missing node.exe, or missing script."""
    files = find_hooks_json_files()
    if not files:
        print("WARN: no openai-codex hooks.json files found", file=sys.stderr)
        return 0
    failures = []
    if not pathlib.Path(NODE_EXE_PATH).exists():
        failures.append(f"NODE_EXE missing: {NODE_EXE_PATH}")
    for hj in files:
        try:
            d = json.loads(hj.read_text())
        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
            failures.append(f"{hj}: json parse error: {e}")
            continue
        for evt, blocks in d.get("hooks", {}).items():
            for block in blocks:
                for h in block.get("hooks", []):
                    cmd = h.get("command", "")
                    if "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}" in cmd:
                        failures.append(f"{hj}: {evt}: ${{CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}} in command")
                    if "/z/" in cmd or "\\z\\" in cmd:
                        failures.append(f"{hj}: {evt}: POSIX /z/ or \\z\\ in command")
                    m = re.search(r'"([A-Za-z]:\\[^"]+\.mjs)"', cmd)
                    if m and not pathlib.Path(m.group(1)).exists():
                        failures.append(f"{hj}: {evt}: script missing at {m.group(1)}")
    if failures:
        print("FAIL:", file=sys.stderr)
        for f in failures:
            print(f"  - {f}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1
    print(f"OK: {len(files)} hooks.json clean")
    return 0


# ============================================================================
# Wave 80 P#3 — patches for openai-codex plugin .mjs source files (issues #191 + #245).
# Cite: codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.91-0.96 verdict at
#       Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/state/codex_consult_wave80_deep_audit_OUT.txt
# Patch design: Z:/claude-sota-installed/tmp/fleet-wave80-deferred-2026-05-08.md TASK B.
# Idempotent: each patch checks for a sentinel marker before re-applying.
# Targets BOTH marketplaces/openai-codex/plugins/codex/scripts/ AND
#         cache/openai-codex/codex/1.0.4/scripts/ paths (verified byte-equal).
# ============================================================================

WAVE80_191_SENTINEL = "// Wave 80 P#191"
WAVE80_245_SENTINEL = "// Wave 80 P#245"


def find_mjs_files(rel_path):
    """Discover .mjs files at rel_path under both runtime cache and marketplaces."""
    results = []
    for runtime in ["Z:/claude-sota", "Z:/claude-sota-installed"]:
        cache_root = pathlib.Path(f"{runtime}/.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex")
        if cache_root.is_dir():
            for ver_dir in sorted(cache_root.iterdir()):
                if ver_dir.is_dir():
                    cand = ver_dir / "scripts" / rel_path
                    if cand.exists():
                        results.append(cand)
        mp = pathlib.Path(
            f"{runtime}/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/openai-codex/plugins/codex/scripts/{rel_path}"
        )
        if mp.exists():
            results.append(mp)
    return results


def patch_191_stop_review_gate_hook(verbose=True):
    """Issue #191 — move stopReviewGate fast-exit BEFORE blocking readHookInput().

    Today: function main() reads stdin synchronously (readHookInput) BEFORE
    checking config.stopReviewGate, so disabled hooks still pay the stdin-block
    cost. Patch: relocate the !config.stopReviewGate fast-exit before the
    blocking read.

    Cite (TIER-1-DIRECT): openai/codex-plugin-cc HEAD 807e03ac plugins/codex/
    scripts/stop-review-gate-hook.mjs.
    """
    files = find_mjs_files("stop-review-gate-hook.mjs")
    rewrites = 0
    skips = 0
    for fp in files:
        text = fp.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        if WAVE80_191_SENTINEL in text:
            if verbose:
                print(f"SKIP P#191 (sentinel present): {fp}")
            skips += 1
            continue
        old_block = (
            "function main() {\n"
            "  const input = readHookInput();\n"
            "  const cwd = input.cwd || process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd();\n"
            "  const workspaceRoot = resolveWorkspaceRoot(cwd);\n"
            "  const config = getConfig(workspaceRoot);\n"
            "\n"
            "  const jobs = sortJobsNewestFirst(filterJobsForCurrentSession(listJobs(workspaceRoot), input));\n"
            "  const runningJob = jobs.find((job) => job.status === \"queued\" || job.status === \"running\");\n"
            "  const runningTaskNote = runningJob\n"
            "    ? `Codex task ${runningJob.id} is still running. Check /codex:status and use /codex:cancel ${runningJob.id} if you want to stop it before ending the session.`\n"
            "    : null;\n"
            "\n"
            "  if (!config.stopReviewGate) {\n"
            "    logNote(runningTaskNote);\n"
            "    return;\n"
            "  }\n"
        )
        new_block = (
            "function main() {\n"
            "  // Wave 80 P#191 — fast-exit BEFORE blocking readHookInput() when gate is disabled.\n"
            "  // readHookInput() calls fs.readFileSync(0) which blocks indefinitely on stdin.\n"
            "  // Disabled-gate sessions should not pay that cost.\n"
            "  // Cite: openai/codex-plugin-cc HEAD 807e03ac plugins/codex/scripts/stop-review-gate-hook.mjs.\n"
            "  const earlyCwd = process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd();\n"
            "  const earlyWorkspaceRoot = resolveWorkspaceRoot(earlyCwd);\n"
            "  const earlyConfig = getConfig(earlyWorkspaceRoot);\n"
            "  if (!earlyConfig.stopReviewGate) {\n"
            "    return;\n"
            "  }\n"
            "\n"
            "  const input = readHookInput();\n"
            "  const cwd = input.cwd || process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd();\n"
            "  const workspaceRoot = resolveWorkspaceRoot(cwd);\n"
            "  const config = getConfig(workspaceRoot);\n"
            "\n"
            "  const jobs = sortJobsNewestFirst(filterJobsForCurrentSession(listJobs(workspaceRoot), input));\n"
            "  const runningJob = jobs.find((job) => job.status === \"queued\" || job.status === \"running\");\n"
            "  const runningTaskNote = runningJob\n"
            "    ? `Codex task ${runningJob.id} is still running. Check /codex:status and use /codex:cancel ${runningJob.id} if you want to stop it before ending the session.`\n"
            "    : null;\n"
            "\n"
            "  if (!config.stopReviewGate) {\n"
            "    logNote(runningTaskNote);\n"
            "    return;\n"
            "  }\n"
        )
        if old_block not in text:
            if verbose:
                print(f"WARN P#191 (block shape changed; manual review): {fp}")
            continue
        backup = fp.with_suffix(fp.suffix + ".pre-wave80-191")
        if not backup.exists():
            backup.write_bytes(fp.read_bytes())
            if verbose:
                print(f"BACKUP P#191: {backup}")
        new_text = text.replace(old_block, new_block, 1)
        fp.write_text(new_text, encoding="utf-8")
        if verbose:
            print(f"PATCHED P#191: {fp}")
        rewrites += 1
    if verbose:
        print(f"P#191: {rewrites} patched, {skips} already correct")
    return rewrites


def patch_245_broker_lifecycle_timeout(verbose=True):
    """Issue #245 — wrap sendBrokerShutdown with 3-second timeout.

    Today: sendBrokerShutdown returns a Promise resolved on data/error/close
    events. If broker accepts connection but never replies, Promise never
    resolves and caller hangs.

    Patch: wrap the inner Promise in Promise.race against a 3000ms setTimeout.

    Cite (TIER-1-DIRECT): openai/codex-plugin-cc HEAD 807e03ac plugins/codex/
    scripts/lib/broker-lifecycle.mjs.
    """
    files = find_mjs_files("lib/broker-lifecycle.mjs")
    rewrites = 0
    skips = 0
    for fp in files:
        text = fp.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        if WAVE80_245_SENTINEL in text:
            if verbose:
                print(f"SKIP P#245 (sentinel present): {fp}")
            skips += 1
            continue
        old_block = (
            "export async function sendBrokerShutdown(endpoint) {\n"
            "  await new Promise((resolve) => {\n"
            "    const socket = connectToEndpoint(endpoint);\n"
            "    socket.setEncoding(\"utf8\");\n"
            "    socket.on(\"connect\", () => {\n"
            "      socket.write(`${JSON.stringify({ id: 1, method: \"broker/shutdown\", params: {} })}\\n`);\n"
            "    });\n"
            "    socket.on(\"data\", () => {\n"
            "      socket.end();\n"
            "      resolve();\n"
            "    });\n"
            "    socket.on(\"error\", resolve);\n"
            "    socket.on(\"close\", resolve);\n"
            "  });\n"
            "}\n"
        )
        new_block = (
            "export async function sendBrokerShutdown(endpoint) {\n"
            "  // Wave 80 P#245 — bound shutdown to 3s; broker may accept connection then hang.\n"
            "  // Cite: openai/codex-plugin-cc HEAD 807e03ac plugins/codex/scripts/lib/broker-lifecycle.mjs.\n"
            "  const shutdownPromise = new Promise((resolve) => {\n"
            "    const socket = connectToEndpoint(endpoint);\n"
            "    socket.setEncoding(\"utf8\");\n"
            "    socket.on(\"connect\", () => {\n"
            "      socket.write(`${JSON.stringify({ id: 1, method: \"broker/shutdown\", params: {} })}\\n`);\n"
            "    });\n"
            "    socket.on(\"data\", () => {\n"
            "      socket.end();\n"
            "      resolve();\n"
            "    });\n"
            "    socket.on(\"error\", resolve);\n"
            "    socket.on(\"close\", resolve);\n"
            "  });\n"
            "  const timeoutPromise = new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 3000));\n"
            "  await Promise.race([shutdownPromise, timeoutPromise]);\n"
            "}\n"
        )
        if old_block not in text:
            if verbose:
                print(f"WARN P#245 (block shape changed; manual review): {fp}")
            continue
        backup = fp.with_suffix(fp.suffix + ".pre-wave80-245")
        if not backup.exists():
            backup.write_bytes(fp.read_bytes())
            if verbose:
                print(f"BACKUP P#245: {backup}")
        new_text = text.replace(old_block, new_block, 1)
        fp.write_text(new_text, encoding="utf-8")
        if verbose:
            print(f"PATCHED P#245: {fp}")
        rewrites += 1
    if verbose:
        print(f"P#245: {rewrites} patched, {skips} already correct")
    return rewrites


if __name__ == "__main__":
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Codex plugin hooks rewriter — Fire 46 durability layer per bog92qxq7 codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION 0.91"
    )
    parser.add_argument("--check", action="store_true",
                        help="Verify all hooks clean; exit 1 if drift detected")
    parser.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true",
                        help="Suppress per-file output")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.check:
        sys.exit(check())
    rc = rewrite(verbose=not args.quiet)
    # Wave 80 P#3 — extend rewriter to patch openai-codex .mjs sources for #191 + #245.
    patch_191_stop_review_gate_hook(verbose=not args.quiet)
    patch_245_broker_lifecycle_timeout(verbose=not args.quiet)
    sys.exit(rc)

Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:44:### Severity gate (mandatory before ACCEPT-WITH-DOC)
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:46:ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is a **hard deny** for any unresolved `high` or `critical` codex severity. (If you want to ship anyway, see the separate MANUAL-OVERRIDE disposition below.)
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:48:For findings at `medium` or `low` severity, ACCEPT-WITH-DOC additionally requires AT LEAST ONE of:
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:53:If neither holds (even at medium severity) → REVERT-AND-REMOVE or kick back to fix-iteration regardless of confidence trend. The severity gate is non-negotiable — iteration count and confidence trend alone cannot bless unresolved material findings.
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:55:### Outcome A — ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (monotone-decline AND severity-gate satisfied)
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:68:**Anti-example (would FAIL the severity gate)**: round 1 high (0.88) → round 2 high (0.80) → round 3 high (0.73) → "stop at round 5 ceiling, monotone-decline, ACCEPT". This would be wrong — severity stayed high; the underlying defect is still material; ACCEPT-WITH-DOC normalizes shipping known security/correctness regressions.
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:70:### Outcome B — REVERT-AND-REMOVE (escalation OR unresolved high-severity)
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:84:When ACCEPT-WITH-DOC's hard-deny on high/critical blocks shipping but the user or named risk-owner explicitly wants the work to land anyway, this is a SEPARATE disposition — not ACCEPT. Future readers must be able to grep for "we shipped a known high/critical defect with explicit acceptance" without that being conflated with "we did our due diligence and bounded the residual risk."
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:111:2. **REVERT-AND-REMOVE** if: confidence escalates between rounds (any round count), OR last-round severity is `high`/`critical` and you have no MANUAL-OVERRIDE
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:112:3. **ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** ONLY if: last-round severity is `medium` or lower AND severity gate passes (concrete verification OR severity downgrade in evidence) AND confidence declined monotonically. Hard deny for any unresolved `high`/`critical`.
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:114:5. **Escalate to user** if: round ≥ 5 with high-severity unresolved finding, no clear REVERT path (e.g., feature has callers we can't easily migrate), and no owner available for MANUAL-OVERRIDE.
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:116:## Anti-pattern: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC on escalating concern
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:120:Lesson: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is only valid when codex's confidence trend confirms convergence. Watch the trend, not just the current round.
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:122:## REVERT-then-recovery trajectory
Z:\claude-sota-installed\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:124:Outcome B (REVERT-AND-REMOVE) is not necessarily terminal for the underlying work. A common recovery shape is: REVERT removes the over-broad surface → a later, narrower fix-forward re-introduces the work with the invariant preserved + scope bounded → that narrower fix-forward converges via Outcome A (monotone-decline ACCEPT). Track the two as separate arcs (the REVERT closes arc 1; the narrower re-introduction opens arc 2) rather than as a single iteration.

Cite class downgraded to TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL with REQUIRED metadata: (a) ISBN/DOI + page + verbatim-quote inline; (b) verifier-identity disclosure (who-verified-when, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-04 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`); (c) explicit `non-automatable: true` marker so review hooks know to defer to human sign-off; (d) **HARD-GATE ELIGIBILITY TEST** *(Round-3 amendment a per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "MUST prefer" soft preference framing — preference is not gateable; eligibility is)*: PROVISIONAL admission is admissible ONLY when DOI landing page + accessible PDF / publisher excerpt / archived scan (license-permitting) was SEARCHED AND UNAVAILABLE. The commit body MUST document the search (which DOI/publisher/archive was queried + return shape — e.g., "searched ACM DOI 10.1145/<doi> 2026-05-05 — paywall-only, no excerpt found; searched Internet Archive — no scan; searched publisher excerpt page — none"). Mere preference is INSUFFICIENT — if any auditable surrogate exists (DOI/PDF/excerpt/scan), use it as the normative source instead of the physical-only book cite. e.g., ACM DOI `https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/<doi>` for ACM papers IS auditable when paywall-free. Secondary aggregator URLs (Wikipedia, `https://pragprog.com/tips/`, `https://martinfowler.com/bliki/<topic>.html`, `c2.com/cgi/wiki`) are VERIFICATION PROXIES only — they help re-verify the primary quote without owning the book physically, but are NOT cited as normative source. **Independent named-reviewer sign-off mandate** *(Round-3 amendment b per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "Lower-confidence reviewer-sign-off mandate" sole-orchestrator framing — sole-orchestrator verification is no longer admissible)*: rule edits citing TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL MUST cite a SECOND verifier-identity BEYOND the orchestrator who replayed the verbatim quote against the source — commit body MUST embed BOTH verifier-identity-1 (the orchestrator, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`) AND verifier-identity-2 (the independent named reviewer, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by <human-name> against same book + same page + same quote]`) with explicit `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd by <name> against <source>]` markers for each. The 2-verifier requirement closes the "self-attestation only" gap that round-2 left open. **`non-automatable: true` operator-discipline mandate (manual-only; no hook enforcement YET)** *(Round-4 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS finding on commit `d07ac71`; supersedes Round-3 amendment c "BLOCKING in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape" claim that was prose-only without hook implementation — codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE caught the policy/enforcement drift: rule text claimed automated BLOCKING but no T3/T4/T6 hook, verdict parser, schema, or test changed. Per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Outcome A monotone-decline path, downgraded prose closes the drift while the enforcement-hook ship is queued separately)*: when a TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL cite is shipped, the operator MUST: (i) embed `non-automatable: true` marker inline; (ii) embed explicit "no automated enforcement, human gate only" disclosure in commit body acknowledging that automated re-verification is impossible AND naming both verifier-identities (per amendment b); (iii) accept residual provenance gap explicitly. The discipline is OPERATOR-MANUAL — NO T3/T4/T6 hook currently parses `non-automatable: true` as BLOCKING. Promotion to mechanical BLOCKING is FORWARD-REF for a separate enforcement-hook ship: `tools/non_automatable_blocking_audit.py` parsing committed rule edits + emitting BLOCKING status in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape + regression tests at `tests/test_non_automatable_blocking_audit.py`. Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:3:description: When iterative codex review (or similar adversarial reviewer) keeps surfacing finer-grained concerns on the same logical surface, decide disposition by confidence trend AND severity gate. Monotone-decline + low/medium severity → ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. Escalation OR unresolved high/critical → REVERT-AND-REMOVE. Owner-accepted high/critical → MANUAL-OVERRIDE (distinct, tracked). Stop the arc at round 5 regardless.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:29:**Critical**: stopping iteration is independent of shipping disposition. Hitting round 5 does NOT mean the surface is safe to ship — it means you've spent enough effort on layer-fix and need to switch to a terminal disposition (ACCEPT-WITH-DOC, REVERT-AND-REMOVE, or escalate to user). The terminal disposition has its OWN gates beyond iteration count.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:35:### Severity gate (mandatory before ACCEPT-WITH-DOC)
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:37:ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is a **hard deny** for any unresolved `high` or `critical` codex severity. No combination of confidence trend, iteration count, or owner signoff can convert an unresolved high/critical finding into ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. (If you want to ship anyway, see the separate MANUAL-OVERRIDE disposition below.)
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:39:For findings at `medium` or `low` severity, ACCEPT-WITH-DOC additionally requires AT LEAST ONE of:
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:44:If neither holds (even at medium severity) → REVERT-AND-REMOVE or kick back to fix-iteration regardless of confidence trend. The severity gate is non-negotiable — iteration count and confidence trend alone cannot bless unresolved material findings.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:46:### Outcome A — ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (monotone-decline AND severity-gate satisfied)
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:59:**Anti-example (would FAIL the severity gate)**: round 1 high (0.88) → round 2 high (0.80) → round 3 high (0.73) → "stop at round 5 ceiling, monotone-decline, ACCEPT". This would be wrong — severity stayed high; the underlying defect is still material; ACCEPT-WITH-DOC normalizes shipping known security/correctness regressions.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:61:### Outcome B — REVERT-AND-REMOVE (escalation OR unresolved high-severity)
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:75:When ACCEPT-WITH-DOC's hard-deny on high/critical blocks shipping but the user or named risk-owner explicitly wants the work to land anyway, this is a SEPARATE disposition — not ACCEPT. Future readers must be able to grep for "we shipped a known high/critical defect with explicit acceptance" without that being conflated with "we did our due diligence and bounded the residual risk."
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:102:2. **REVERT-AND-REMOVE** if: confidence escalates between rounds (any round count), OR last-round severity is `high`/`critical` and you have no MANUAL-OVERRIDE
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:103:3. **ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** ONLY if: last-round severity is `medium` or lower AND severity gate passes (concrete verification OR severity downgrade in evidence) AND confidence declined monotonically. Hard deny for any unresolved `high`/`critical`.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:105:5. **Escalate to user** if: round ≥ 5 with high-severity unresolved finding, no clear REVERT path (e.g., feature has callers we can't easily migrate), and no owner available for MANUAL-OVERRIDE.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:107:## Anti-pattern: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC on escalating concern
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:111:Lesson: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is only valid when codex's confidence trend confirms convergence. Watch the trend, not just the current round.
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:113:## REVERT-then-recovery trajectory
Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:115:Outcome B (REVERT-AND-REMOVE) is not necessarily terminal for the underlying work. A common recovery shape is: REVERT removes the over-broad surface → a later, narrower fix-forward re-introduces the work with the invariant preserved + scope bounded → that narrower fix-forward converges via Outcome A (monotone-decline ACCEPT). Track the two as separate arcs (the REVERT closes arc 1; the narrower re-introduction opens arc 2) rather than as a single iteration.

Cite class downgraded to TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL with REQUIRED metadata: (a) ISBN/DOI + page + verbatim-quote inline; (b) verifier-identity disclosure (who-verified-when, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-04 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`); (c) explicit `non-automatable: true` marker so review hooks know to defer to human sign-off; (d) **HARD-GATE ELIGIBILITY TEST** *(Round-3 amendment a per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "MUST prefer" soft preference framing — preference is not gateable; eligibility is)*: PROVISIONAL admission is admissible ONLY when DOI landing page + accessible PDF / publisher excerpt / archived scan (license-permitting) was SEARCHED AND UNAVAILABLE. The commit body MUST document the search (which DOI/publisher/archive was queried + return shape — e.g., "searched ACM DOI 10.1145/<doi> 2026-05-05 — paywall-only, no excerpt found; searched Internet Archive — no scan; searched publisher excerpt page — none"). Mere preference is INSUFFICIENT — if any auditable surrogate exists (DOI/PDF/excerpt/scan), use it as the normative source instead of the physical-only book cite. e.g., ACM DOI `https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/<doi>` for ACM papers IS auditable when paywall-free. Secondary aggregator URLs (Wikipedia, `https://pragprog.com/tips/`, `https://martinfowler.com/bliki/<topic>.html`, `c2.com/cgi/wiki`) are VERIFICATION PROXIES only — they help re-verify the primary quote without owning the book physically, but are NOT cited as normative source. **Independent named-reviewer sign-off mandate** *(Round-3 amendment b per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "Lower-confidence reviewer-sign-off mandate" sole-orchestrator framing — sole-orchestrator verification is no longer admissible)*: rule edits citing TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL MUST cite a SECOND verifier-identity BEYOND the orchestrator who replayed the verbatim quote against the source — commit body MUST embed BOTH verifier-identity-1 (the orchestrator, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`) AND verifier-identity-2 (the independent named reviewer, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by <human-name> against same book + same page + same quote]`) with explicit `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd by <name> against <source>]` markers for each. The 2-verifier requirement closes the "self-attestation only" gap that round-2 left open. **`non-automatable: true` operator-discipline mandate (manual-only; no hook enforcement YET)** *(Round-4 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS finding on commit `d07ac71`; supersedes Round-3 amendment c "BLOCKING in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape" claim that was prose-only without hook implementation — codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE caught the policy/enforcement drift: rule text claimed automated BLOCKING but no T3/T4/T6 hook, verdict parser, schema, or test changed. Track codex confidence values across the arc. Disposition is determined by BOTH the trend AND a severity gate (iteration ceiling alone cannot bless unresolved material findings).

### Severity gate (mandatory before ACCEPT-WITH-DOC)

ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is a **hard deny** for any unresolved `high` or `critical` codex severity. No combination of confidence trend, iteration count, or owner signoff can convert an unresolved high/critical finding into ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. (If you want to ship anyway, see the separate MANUAL-OVERRIDE disposition below.)

For findings at `medium` or `low` severity, ACCEPT-WITH-DOC additionally requires AT LEAST ONE of:

- **Concrete verification**: a passing probe/test demonstrates the residual risk is actually bounded (e.g., "fuzzed 1000 inputs, no escape", "regression test added at file:line"). Documentation of a threat model is not verification — assertions of boundedness must be empirically backed.
- **Severity downgrade in evidence**: codex's last-round severity field landed at `low` or `info` after the fix iterations, indicating the residual risk is materially smaller than the original concern.

If neither holds (even at medium severity) → REVERT-AND-REMOVE or kick back to fix-iteration regardless of confidence trend. The severity gate is non-negotiable — iteration count and confidence trend alone cannot bless unresolved material findings.

### Outcome A — ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (monotone-decline AND severity-gate satisfied)

Confidence declines monotonically across rounds AND the severity gate passes. Codex is acknowledging your fixes address the concern progressively AND the residual risk is verifiably bounded (or low-severity, or owner-accepted).

```
Round 1: 0.88 (primary, severity high)
Round 2: 0.80 (derivative, severity medium)
Round 3: 0.73 (residual, severity low)  ← severity drops below high
Round 4: APPROVE
```

Disposition: ship a doc-comment ACCEPT in the patch site explaining threat model + verification reference + ROI + upstream issue queue. Future workers see the explicit acceptance; arc closes cleanly.

**Anti-example (would FAIL the severity gate)**: round 1 high (0.88) → round 2 high (0.80) → round 3 high (0.73) → "stop at round 5 ceiling, monotone-decline, ACCEPT". This would be wrong — severity stayed high; the underlying defect is still material; ACCEPT-WITH-DOC normalizes shipping known security/correctness regressions.

### Outcome B — REVERT-AND-REMOVE (escalation OR unresolved high-severity)

Confidence reverses between rounds on the **same concern**. This is codex saying "your disposition didn't address my actual point" — an ACCEPT comment doesn't fix a security bug, and codex is escalating to ensure you don't ship the unfixed surface.

```
Round 1: 0.82 (primary)

## Pattern C — Verifier-precision in T2 close-the-loop (n=2 evidence, iter-41 codification)

Codex T2 close-the-loop reviews (post-commit verification fired manually
after Pattern A apply) MUST use the **exact runtime invocation** the fix
targets. Generic-shape verifications produce false-negative classifications
when the verifier's chosen test command differs from the hook's actual call
shape.

### Evidence trail

| iter | Fix target | Verifier's test invocation | Defect class | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| iter-37 T2 (`bn75gm2ns`) | `gitnexus_pre_edit_impact_guard.py` adds `--repo claude-sota` to `gitnexus impact <symbol>` argv | codex tested `gitnexus status --repo claude-sota` | `unknown option '--repo'` for `status`; correct for `impact` — wrong subcommand | iter-38 T2 used the simulated hook payload directly + got `[gitnexus-impact:debug] impactedCount=0 (<= 10, clean)` |
| iter-38 T2 (`btpn2w7q7`) | T3/T4 hook `_SCHEMA_PATH` resolves to vendored `.claude/schemas/` at module import | codex tested `python -c "from codex_postcommit_review import _SCHEMA_PATH; ..."` from arbitrary cwd | `ModuleNotFoundError`; runtime is correct because hook executes from `.claude/hooks/scripts/` with parents[3] resolution; verifier-context defective | iter-38 T2 added explicit `sys.path.insert(0, '.claude/hooks/scripts')` and re-probed — got expected absolute path + True |

### Discipline: T2 close-the-loop prompt MUST include exact runtime invocation

Mandatory section in T2 consult prompts (extends `cross-model-consensus.md`
HARD GATE):

```
LIVE SMOKE PRE-COMMIT (or pre-fire if T1):
  $ <exact subcommand + flags + cwd-context the hook actually uses>
  → <expected output verbatim>

VERIFIER MUST USE THIS EXACT INVOCATION TO TEST.
Generic-shape probes (different subcommand, different cwd, different
argv) produce false-negatives — verifier-context-defective tests
classify a working fix as "still suspect" or "needs-revision."
```

### Why this matters

False-negative T2 verdicts cause two compounding harms:
1. **Wasted iter** — Pattern A fix-forward triggered to "address" a
   non-existent defect, creating churn instead of progress.
2. **Lost trust** — operator who reads "B7 still active" after iter-37
   shipped the fix may incorrectly assume the fix didn't work, leading
   to redundant duplicate-fix attempts.
